I, Shri Udai Vir Singh Assistant Sub Inspector GM 


PART [-.86८. | THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) 75 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


In response to the barbaric terrorist attack in Pahalgam on 22nd April, 2025, India launched ‘Operation Sindoor’ on 
the night of 7-8 May, 2025 which involved precision strikes targeting nine terrorists’ logistics training sites in Pakistan and 
Pakistan occupied Jammu and Kashmir. 


In retaliation, Pakistan resorted to heavy firing, shelling and drone attacks on BSF posts across the International Border 
in Jammu region. During the morning of !0 May 2025 at about 0525 hrs, in a premeditated and unprovoked escalation aimed 
at avenging “Operation Sindoor,” Pakistan Rangers alongwith regulars launched an intense and heavy assault on several 
Forward Duty Points (FDPs) and Border Out Posts (BOPs) of !20 Bn BSF in Jammu Sector. AOR of 20 Bn BSF bore the 
brunt of this onslaught, coming under concentrated fire from Pakistan side. BSF troops in morchas of AOR, 20 Bn BSF 
constantly fired upon enemy posts using all weapons at their disposal. 


Later in the evening at around 700 hrs, Pak- Rangers once again initiated heavy fire, combining 82 mm mortar shells 
and flat trajectory weapons targeting Indian BOPs and border villages. On 0 May 2025, ASI Udai Vir Singh was deployed at 
BOP Jabowal, at FDP No. 06, where a MMG was also positioned, he was assigned the mission to destroy a night vision 
surveillance camera with Thermal Image installed at the Pakistani Post Jumat, thereby denying the adversary real-time 
surveillance of our BOP and troops movements. 


At 0525 hrs, Pakistan launched a coordinated fire assault on our BOPs and FDPs, using heavy fire of high and flat- 
trajectory weapons. Amidst this hostile engagement, he bravely executed his mission and successfully destroyed the enemy 
surveillance camera. Enraged by the strike, the enemy unleashed heavy-caliber fire on FDP No. 6 deploying HMGs in a direct 
fire role. During the intense barrage, ASI Udai Vir Singh, sustained shrapnel wound to his upper lip from HMG splinters and 
was bleeding profusely. Refusing evacuation, he maintained control of his FDP and continued engaging the enemy position 
from which the HMG fire was being directed. His precise MMG fire neutralized the enemy HMG nest, rendering it silent and 
ensuring uninterrupted domination form our side. 


ASI Udai Vir Singh suffered life threatening injury and profuse bleeding due to the sustained shrapnel wound to his 
upper lip. Yet, despite the devastating nature of his injury, he remained conscious, stabilized himself and insisted that he 
maintained control of his FDP and continued engaging the enemy position from which the HMG fire was being directed. Even 
while being seriously wounded, ASI Udai Vir Singh motivated his fellow soldiers exhibiting tremendous courage. His 
exceptional valor under fire enabled the continued retaliatory response from the FDP, playing a pivotal role in repelling the 
enemy and preserving the post’s offensive and defensive capability. Later, ASI Udai Vir Singh underwent 


treatment for wound of his upper lip at Military Hospital, Jammu but his spirit of patriotism and commitment to his 
motherland is undiminished. Even after being evacuated to the Military Hospital, his resilience never waned. He greeted visitors 
with warmth and expressed a strong desire to return to the line of duty an enduring symbol of bravery, mental strength, and 
loyalty to the force and the nation. 


In this operation, Shri Udai Vir Singh, Assistant Sub Inspector of Border Security Force displayed conspicuous 
gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 0/05/2025.

l. Vyas Dev Sub Inspector GM 

2. Suddi Rabha Constable GM 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


In response to the barbaric terrorist attack in Pahalgam on 22 April 2025, India launched ‘Operation Sindoor’ on the 
night of 7-8 May 2025, which involved precision strikes targeting nine terrorists’ logistics/training, sites in Pakistan and 
Pakistan occupied Jammu and Kashmir. In retaliation, Pakistan resorted to heavy firing, shelling and drone attacks on BSF 
posts across the International Border (IB) in Jammu region. 


752 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) [PART I—Sec. 


During the intervening night of 09-l0 May 2025, in a premeditated and unprovoked escalation aimed at avenging 
“Ops Sindoor," Pakistan Rangers along with regulars launched an intense and heavy assault on several Forward Duty Points 
and Border Out Posts (BOPs) of 07 Bn BSF in Jammu Sector. BOP Sushil bore the brunt of this onslaught, coming under 
concentrated fire from Pakistani posts Putwal Morcha, Putwal, and Chaprar. The-post was pounded with a long barrage of 82 
mm mortar shells and heavy machine gun fire, threatening its very defensive integrity. BSF troops in morchas of BOP Sushil 
constantly fired upon enemy-posts using all weapons at their disposal all throughout that night. 


Around 0500 hrs, during a brief pause, SI Vyas Dev and Ct Suddi Rabha were detailed by company commander to 
replenish ammunition to frontline troops in morchas to ensure readiness for any renewed enemy attack. This entailed 
tremendous risk and exposure to sudden enemy shelling. Notwithstanding the duo of SI Vyas Dev and Ct Suddi Rabha took up 
this onerous task with fortitude. 


The two of them drew ammunition from the magazine of the company in BOP Sushil and started moving to the 
morchas in a bid to distribute it to the needy troops. As they were performing this risk prone mission, shelling suddenly began 
from the enemy side. However, SI Vyas Dev and Ct Suddi Rabha realising the importance of their assignment gallantly 
continued in the face of certain death. 


They replenished ammunition in AD gun, MMG morcha and took stock of the AGS which developed malfunction. 
While en route to a morcha behind the mahila barrack, an enemy 82 mm mortar bomb suddenly exploded near them causing 
grievous-multiple splinter injuries to both. 


SI Vyas Dev suffered life threatening injuries and profuse bleeding due to the multiple severe splinter wounds. Yet, 
despite the devastating nature of his injuries, he remained conscious, stabilized himself and insisted that the ammunition be 
delivered to the forward morchas before allowing himself to be evacuated. Even while being seriously wounded, SI Vyas Dev 
motivated his fellow soldiers exhibiting tremendous courage. His exceptional valor under fire enabled the continued retaliatory 
response from the post, playing a pivotal role in repelling the enemy and preserving the post's offensive and defensive 
capability: Later, SI Vyas Dev underwent a traumatic amputation of his left leg at Military Hospital, Jammu but his spirit of 
patriotism and commitment to his motherland is undiminished. Even after being evacuated to the Military Hospital, his 
resilience never waned. He greeted visitors with warmth and expressed a strong desire to returned the line of duty -an enduring 
symbol of bravery, mental strength, and loyalty to the force and the nation. 


CT Suddi Rabha, equally resolute and courageous, stood shoulder to shoulder with his commander SI Vyas Dev during 
the critical ammunition replenishment operation. He sustained severe blast trauma-deep lacerations in the perineal region, 
injuries to both thighs, the left leg and right ankle. Despite the immense pain and life- threatening wounds, CT Suddi Rabha 
refused to yield. Alongside his injured comrade, he ensured that the ammunition reached the forward morchas, directly 
contributing to the destruction of enemy fire positions. His calm demeanor under pressure and commitment to duty inspired 
everyone present at the post. 


In this operation, S/Shri Vyas Dev, Sub Inspector and Suddi Rabha, Constable of Border Security Force displayed 
conspicuous gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023.

|6. | Basavaraja Shivappa Sunkada Constable GM 


PART [-.86८. | THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) 753 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


In response to the barbaric terrorist attack in Pahalgam on 22 April 2025, India launched 'Operation Sindoor’ on the 
night of 7-8 May 2025, which involved precision strikes targeting nine terrorists’ logistics/training, sites in Pakistan and 
Pakistan occupied Jammu and Kashmir. In retaliation, Pakistan resorted to heavy firing, shelling and drone attacks on BSF 
posts across the International Border in Jammu region. 


On 09 May 2025 at around 2!5 hrs, Pakistani Post Afzal Shaheed initiated unprovoked heavy machine gun fire on 
BSF’s Border Out Post (BOP) AMK in Jammu district. The Company Commander of BOP AMK promptly informed the 
Commandant, 07 Bn at BOP Sushil, who ordered a befitting response and alerted all coy Commanders to prepare for retaliation. 
Shortly thereafter, adjoining Pakistani Posts Chota Chak and New Chumbian joined the attack drawing retaliation from BSF’s 
BOPs Ommen, Makwal Forward, and Kharkola. 


BSF’s BOP Kharkola was being commanded by SI Mohd. Imteyaj. Apart from him, on the intervening night of 09- 
0 May 2025, HC Brij Mohan Singh, CT Deepak Chingakham, CT Depeswar Barman, CT Bhupendra Bajpai, CT Rajan Kumar 
and CT Basavaraja Shivappa Sunkada arid an AC under training Abhishek Srivastav, all of 07 Bn BSF amongst others were 
deployed at BOP Kharkola. 


BOP Kharkola which is barely situated 200 meters from the International Border came under intense fifing involving 
heavy mortar shelling and flat trajectory fire from automatic weapons from Pakistani posts across throughout the intervening 
night of 09/I.0 May 2025. BSF troops under the able command of Post Commander SI Mohd. Imteyaj gave a befitting reply 
from BOP Kharkola and its forward duty posts. 


Amidst this hostile engagement, multiple enemy drones were observed intruding over BOP Kharkola, suspected to be 
conducting aerial reconnaissance and attempting to drop explosive payloads or guide mortar fire, posing grave threats to 
personnel deployed in the BOP and its installations. At around 0500 hours on 0 May 2025, Inspector Rajveer Singh Jat, 
Officiating Company Commander stationed at Command-and-Control Centre of BOP Mangral, observed this rogue drone 
activity and passed instructions to SI Mohd. Imteyaj, Post Commander BOP Kharkola to remain alert. 


Upon hearing, humming sound of drone over his BOP and sensing imminent danger to the lives of his men from this 
aerial threat, SI Mohd. Imteyaj tactically moved out of his command bunker and spotted two Pakistani drones hovering over 
the BOP. He immediately summoned a Light Machine Gun (LMG) shouting "drone drone, LMG jaldi lao" to neutralize the 
drones. Responding swiftly, HC Brij Mohan Singh, Ct Deepak Chingakham, and Ct Depeswar Barman rushed with weapons. 


CT Bhupendra Bajpai, CT Rajan Kumar and CT Basavaraja Shivappa Sunkada from their Automatic Grenade 
Launching System (AGLS) morcha in the BOP also saw the drones. SI Mohd. Imteyaj took the LMG from Ct Deepak and 
fired, successfully neutralizing one drone, while the other retreated. Soon, another rogue drone appeared. Ct Deepak took back 
the LMG and opened fire, joined by SI Mohd. Imteyaj, HC Brij Mohan Singh, and Ct Depeswar Barman. 


However, within no time an enemy 82 mm mortar shell, perhaps dropped by the drone, exploded just outside the 
AGLS morcha, causing grievous splinter injuries to SI Mohd. Imteyaj, HC Brij Mohan Singh, Ct Deepak Chingakham, and Ct 
Depeswar Barman. 


HC Brij Mohan Singh, who had earlier responded to the drone threat with exceptional alertness and battlefield 
initiative, got grievously injured, while attempting to engage enemy drones. The blast caused multiple splinter injuries and 
severe maxillofacial trauma, including a near complete fracture of the mandible and maxilla. Despite being rendered immobile 
and incapacitated, HC Brij Mohan Singh displayed indomitable courage, lying grievously wounded without panic defying deep 
pain and refusing to compromise morale of his fellow soldiers. While in the ICU for nearly two weeks, unable to speak due to 
2 cm bone loss, he continued to show mental resilience, saluting officers and comrades from his bed, embodying stoic 
discipline and battlefield honour. 


Constable Depeswar Barman during the neutralization of enemy drones, suffered grievous blast injuries, including 
perineal trauma and multiple splinter wounds to his neck and lower limbs. Undeterred by unbearable pain and danger to his 
life, Ct Depeswar Barman continued retaliatory fire with his LMG and thwarted aerial threats while inspiring his comrades. 
Displaying immense composure under fire, he assisted in evacuating critically injured personnel and ensuring continuity of 
defensive operations under severe enemy engagement. 


Constable Bhupendra Bajpai sustained splinter injuries during the mortar explosion when enemy drones were being 
neutralized over BOP Khakola. Despite his own wounds, he responded without hesitation to the evacuation call of Shri 
Abhishek Srivastav, AC. Displaying exceptional courage, he helped in evacuating his injured colleagues under heavy enemy 
fire, risking his own life to ensure their safety. 


Constable Rajan Kumar, though injured by splinters, displayed remarkable bravery in the heat of this battle. When 
called upon, he immediately joined the rescue effort, exposing himself to enemy fire and helping lift his fellow injured soldiers 
across open ground to the safety of the overhead protected morcha. 


Constable Basavaraja Shivappa Sunkada was also wounded in the explosion at the same time. He exhibited 
unwavering determination and selflessness ignoring his own blast injuries, he played a critical role in the evacuation, physically 


754 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) [PART I—Sec. 


pulling injured teammates to safety under continuous shelling. His actions reflected extraordinary valor and deep camaraderie 
in combat. 


Shri Abhishek Srivastav, AC was posted in BOP Kharkola as part of his on-job-training. He was present in the 
command bunker with post commander SI Mohd. Imteyaj in that eventful period closely learning the nitty- gritties and getting 
battle inoculated. When SI Mohd. Imteyaj stirred out the bunker to neutralize enemy drones, this young officer heard some 
loud explosion moments after. Without caring for his own safety, he rushed to a badly wounded SI Mohd. Imteyaj and the other 
injured personnel. Under those trying circumstances, AC Abhishek Srivastav managed to organize evacuation of all the injured 
thereby saving the lives of his fellow men. He demonstrated unwavering courage, initiative and presence of mind.

No. 49—Pres/2025—The President of India is pleased to award Medal for Gallantry (GM) to the under mentioned 
personnel of Border Security Force (BSF):— 


Sl. No. Name of the Awardee Rank at the time of Action Medal awarded 
S/Shri 
Rajappa B T Assistant Sub Inspector GM 

2; Manohar Xalxo Constable GM 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


In response to the barbaric terrorist attack in Pahalgam on 22 April 2025, India launched ‘Operation Sindoor’ on the 
night of 7-8 May 2025, which involved precision strikes targeting nine terrorists’ logistics/training, sites in Pakistan and 
Pakistan occupied Jammu and Kashmir. In retaliation, Pakistan resorted to heavy firing, shelling and drone attacks on BSF 
posts across the International Border in Jammu region. 


During the intervening night-of 09-l0 May 2025, in a premeditated and unprovoked escalation aimed at avenging 
“Ops Sindoor,” Pakistan Rangers along with regulars launched, an intense and heavy assault on several Forward Duty Points 
and Border Out Posts (BOPs) of !65 Bn BSF in Jammu Sector. BOPs Karotana Khurd, Karotana Forward and Suchetgarh bore 
the brunt of this onslaught, coming under concentrated fire from Pakistani posts Jamshed, Malane and Kasira. The post was 
pounded with a continuous barrage of 82 mm mortar shells and heavy machine gun fire, threatening its very defensive integrity. 
BSF troops in morchas of BOP Karotana Khurd, Karotana Forward and Suchetgarh constantly fired upon enemy posts using 
all weapons at their disposal all throughout that night. 


In the morning hours of !0 May 2025, Pakistan again started firing with mortars and flat trajectory weapons. Forceful 
reply was being given by BSF’s ASI Rajappa B T and CT Manohar Xalxo, deployed at BOP Karotana Khurd of !65 Bn BSF 
which is situated approx. 500 meters away from International Boundary. At about 0740 hrs, BOP morchas informed the Post 
Commander that they are likely to exhaust available ammunition of Automatic Grenade Launching System and replenishment 
was essential to retaliate. The company Commander detailed these two personnel namely ASI Rajappa B T and CT Manohar 
Xalxo for providing ammunition to the morchas Jrom the company kote. 


In order to save lives of their colleagues and without caring for their personal life and safety, Kote Commander ASI 
Rajappa B T and CT Manohar Xalxo moved towards the magazine where ammunition was kept. They transported the 
ammunition once to the AGS & other morchas for fighting and giving befitting reply to counterpart/enemy during shelling of 
counterpart to defend their post & men. However, at about 0750 hrs; when they moved tactically to supply additional 
ammunition, one 82 mm mortar bomb fired by Pakistan exploded near the magazine causing multiple grievous splinter injuries 
to ASI Rajappa B T on his right side of his body and CT Manohar Xalxo also suffered injuries on right hand thumb and ring 
finger with splinters. Inspite of injuries both of them continued to carry out the assigned, task in face of certain death exhibiting 
unmatched gallantry. Later, they were evacuated to Military Hospital, Jammu. 


ASI Rajappa B T suffered serious splinters injuries in providing critical assistance to his fellow troopers in the face of 
fire without caring of his personal safety and saved the life of others who could counter the enemy fire effectively. He also 
ensured that despite suffering from injuries, he pulled the ammunition boxes to a safe place thus preventing a major mishap. In 
a show of exemplary bravery, initiative and presence of mind, his action helped put the enemy on the back foot. 


PART [-.86०. | THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) 755 


CT Manohar Xalxo suffered splinters injuries in combating the enemy and in the face of fire without caring of his 
personal safety. He bravely assisted his commander in providing ammunition supplies even after suffering from splinters 
injuries. His actions were vital in countering enemy fire. 


In this operation, S/Shri Rajappa B T, Assistant Sub Inspector and Manohar Xalxo, Constable of Border Security 
Force displayed conspicuous gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 0/05/2025.

No. |50—Pres/2025—The President of India is pleased to award Medal for Gallantry (GM) to the under mentioned 
personnel of Central Reserve Police Force (CRPF):— 


SI. No. Name of the Awardee Rank at the time of Action Medal awarded 
S/Shri 

l. Santosh Kumar Pal Second-In-Command GM 

2. Abhin Kumar Majhi Constable GM 

3. Lekhraj Chetry Constable GM 

4. Vivek Kumar Constable GM 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


On 28 December 2022, a specific input received from SP Lohardaga about the presence of CPI Maoist dasta of RCM 
Ravindra Ganjhu near vill Korgo, PS Bagru Distt Lohardaga, Jharkhand. Immediately, a joint operation was meticulously 
planned consisting troops of 58 CRPF under command Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, 2 IC and troops of Jharkhand Police/SAT 
under command of Sh Deepak Kumar Pandey, Sr DySP (Ops). Joint troops moved out at around 030 hrs by Motorcycle on 
29 December 2022. 


Due to the imminent threat of pressure-triggered IEDs, the troops after reaching the dropping point near vill Gumiatoli, 
started a cross-country tactical movement. As they approached the target area, an outer cut off was established by SAT-9]. 
The remaining party continued their advance towards the target area, negotiating natural obstacles. Upon nearing the suspected 
area and to effectively cordon it off, the troops were divided into two teams. Team-| with SAT-3 along with F coy of 58, 
covering the right Hank and Team-2, under command Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, 2IC, along with QAT 58 and DAP, advanced 
from the left flank to conduct a thorough search of the probable enemy area. 


At dawn, as a small strike component of Team-|, advanced tactically, a Naxal sentry detected their movement and 
started firing towards them. The troops challenged the armed Naxals to surrender, but the Naxals intensified their fire. 
Strategically, the troops retaliated, causing the Naxal group to alter their positions. Taking advantage of the dense vegetation, 
the Naxals maneuvered towards the other side where Team-2 had already assumed their positions. To outflank the enemy, Shri 
Santosh Kumar Pal, 2 IC, swiftly and tactically repositioned his team and positioned himself in front of the assault group of 
QAT 58. At the critical moment when two Naxals from Dasta entered from the right side, they were promptly challenged by 
the nearest QAT personnel within the cordon. In response, the Naxals opened fire on this party and attempted to move in two 
different directions. 


Ct Lekhraj Chetry chased one of the Naxals, while other personnel alerted the commander about the movements of 
the other Naxal from his location. Reacting quickly to the situation, Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, 2 IC, ordered his team not to open 
fire, considering that the QAT members were positioned behind the Naxals. This strategic decision was vital to maintain the 
element of surprise for the remaining party. Recognizing the direction of the Naxals' movement, Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, 2 IC, 
directed the buddy Ct Vivek Kumar to advance from the left flank to provide support to Ct Lekhraj Chetiy, who was chasing 
one Naxal. Simultaneously, he instructed the right flank buddy Ct Abhin Kumar Majhi to crawl and position them near the wall 
of the hut for better tactical cover. 


During this intense engagement, one of the Naxals advancing towards the hut spotted Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, 2 IC, 
and opened indiscriminate fire in his direction. Fortunately, he narrowly escaped and undeterred by the hail of Naxal fire, 
Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, 2 IC, bravely retaliated, inflicting injuries on the Naxal and compelling him to take cover near the 
hut. Meanwhile, Ct Abhin Kumar Majhi closed in on the hut, reached a strategic position for cover and opened fire, due to 
which Naxal could not breach the cordon and fell down near hut due to severe bullet injuries. 


756 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) [PART I—Sec. 


On the other side, Ct Vivek Kumar and Ct Lekhraj Chetry continued to advance towards the other Naxal, adopting 
fire and move tactics from the left and right sides. Amidst the exchange of gunfire from multiple directions, the Naxal got 
panicked. Suddenly, he changed his direction, attempting to climb uphill. However, due to the weight of his heavy backpack, 
his climbing attempt failed. As the Naxal momentarily ceased firing to use his hands for climbing, his weapon slipped from his 
grasp. Recognizing the opportunity, both Constables swiftly closed in on the Naxal from their respective directions. As the 
Naxal moved backward to retrieve his weapon, he was challenged by Ct Vivek Kumar to raise his hands. In a split second and 
under the commander’s direction, Ct Lekhraj Chetry bravely apprehended the fleeing Naxal from behind. Consequently, one 
of the Naxals was captured alive. 


The two apprehended Naxals were identified as Chandrabhan Pahan (SZC) and Govind Birajia (SZC), both of whom 
had a reward of 5 lakhs on their heads. After providing initial medical aid to the injured Naxal, Chandrabhan Pahan, on the 
spot, he was promptly evacuated to Sadar Hospital, Lohardaga but, he succumbed to his injuries. 


During the on spot interrogations by both team commanders, the apprehended Naxal, Govind Birijia, disclosed the 
names of other fleeing Naxals who had taken cover in thick vegetation. He also revealed the presence of arms and ammunition 
dump in the Korgo forest area and IEDs dump in the Guni forest area. In response to this valuable information, QAT 58 under 
the command of Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, 2IC, and SAT-l9l under the command of Shri Deepak Kumar Pandey swiftly 
proceeded to vill Guni, PS Sarendag, for the recovery of 200 IEDs, leaving F- 58 and SAT-3 under the command of Shri 
Ashiq Ali at Korgo to secure the area housing the arms and ammunition dump. 


Following the successful demolition of 200 IEDs and 50 meters of Cordex wire, the troops returned to Korgo, and 
the operation continued until the recovery of the arms and ammunition dump the next day. This operation yielded significant 
results, as | No SLR, | No INSAS, 2 No 303 rifles, | No 306 semi-automatic rifle, and 597 rounds of ammunition of various 
calibers were recovered. 


In this operation, S/Shri Santosh Kumar Pal, Second-In-Command, Abhin Kumar Majhi, Constable, Lekhraj Chetry, 
Constable and Vivek Kumar, Constable of Central Reserve Police Force displayed conspicuous gallantry, courage and devotion 
to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 29/]2/2022. 


(File No. 020/52/2023-PMA)

Appreciating the urgency of situation, Shri Ashok Kumar coordinated with the DIGP (Ops) CRPF Bijapur Range and the SP 
of Bijapur District to launch an immediate evacuation operation. 


By 950 hours, six teams from 2!0 CoBRA. comprised of personnel from Sarkeguda and Pegrapalli camps, were 
deployed to secure the route between Basaguda and Pegrapalli. Despite possibility of potential ambush threats, the CoBRA 
troops, led by Shri Ashok Kumar, undertook the rescue of injured Police officer despite the grave dangers.

site. As a result of conspicuous bravery and sharp tactical maneuvering of the troops, the critically injured Sub inspector was 
successfully evacuated.

During the night of 07/08 December 202, SSP Shopian shared actionable intelligence regarding the presence of 2-3 
armed terrorists in Chek Cholan village, Shopian, J&K. Based on this specific intelligence input, a joint cordon & search 
operation was planned in a swift manner by Shri Kumar Navin, 2-IC of 78 CRPF with 34 RR and Jammu & Kashmir Police/ 
Special Operation Group, in the reported area. As per plan, joint teams of !78 CRPF, 34 RR and J&K Police reached target 
area at approximately 000 Hrs.

62, where terrorists were hiding. He ensured safety of the surrounding area by securing adjacent houses and strategically, set 
up a fire base at House No. 6], directly facing the target house, enabling a coordinated and well executed operational response. 


At first light, an appeal for surrender to the terrorists was issued. The terrorists, instead of acceding to the appeal, 
launched an indiscriminate and aggressive attack on the security forces. The joint team, exercising restraint and precision, 
retaliated in self-defense. During this exchange, one terrorist attempted to escape towards a nearby drain. In a display of 
exceptional skill and coordination, Shri Teja Ram Choudhary, Asstt. Comdt., along with CT Ganesh Chand Jat and CT Jeet 
Tanti, eliminated the fleeing terrorist, ensuring the intactness of the cordon. 


Despite the neutralization of one terrorist, random firing from the target house continued. To counter the escalating 
situation, a BP Backhoe Loader Machine was utilized to demolish the boundary wall of the house, enhancing visibility and 
reducing the terrorists' tactical advantage. However, the terrorist retaliated fiercely, targeting the positions at House Nos. 6], 
where the SFs were deployed. 


Amidst intense gunfire, Shri Kumar Navin, 2-IC, and CT Rafiq Ahmad Lone fearlessly engaged the attackers, 
neutralizing a second terrorist within the house. Their unwavering focus and precise retaliatory fire ensured maintenance of an 
upper hand by the joint forces. As a result, the remaining terrorist was subsequently neutralized after a coordinated assault by 
the CRPF teams.

Lashkar-e-Taiba (LeT) terrorist outfit, alongwith their arms & ammunition thereby effectively neutralizing their nefarious 
plans.

Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


During the intervening night of 09/0 October 2023, a joint counter-terrorism operation was launched in Alishahpora, 
PS-Zainapora, District Shopian (J&K), based on a specific intelligence input shared by SSP Shopian about the presence of 2-3 
heavily armed terrorists in the area. 


As per the ops plan, ensuring swift and coordinated action; joint teams were deployed to cordon two houses and a 
religious structure (DARASGAH) suspected of harboring terrorists. Civilians in the vicinity were tactfully evacuated to safety, 
though none confirmed the presence of terrorists. Realizing the imminent challenge posed by the security forces, the terrorists 
readied themselves for confrontation. 


The joint teams, led by Shri Teja Ram Choudhary, Assistant Commandant of 78 CRPF began advancing toward the 
target house. Positioned at the village corner with an orchard nearby, the single-story house posed a high risk for potential 
escape attempts. As the teams approached, the terrorists unleashed indiscriminate fire, attempting to disrupt the advance of the 
security forces. Demonstrating exceptional tactical acumen, the teams avoiding casualties, secured defensive positions, and 
retaliated effectively. 


To pinpoint the terrorists' exact location, a drone was deployed, confirming the presence of two armed militants at the 
doorstep of the target house. To prevent their escape into the dense orchard or nearby forest, the cordon was tightened, firebases 
were reinforced and nearby structures like house were strategically occupied by joint forces. Despite repeated surrender appeals 
made by the J&K Police, the terrorists responded with heavy gunfire, leading to a fierce gun battle. 


During the encounter, one terrorist attempted to escape by firing indiscriminately toward the southeast of the cordon. 
Displaying remarkable composure and exceptional operational skills, Shri Teja Ram Choudhary, AC and his team swiftly 
neutralized him with precise retaliatory fire. Meanwhile, the second terrorist, injured while crawling toward the forest, initially 
managed to conceal himself but revealed his position at first light while trying to flee again. He was subsequently neutralized 
by CT Ganesh Chand Jat and CT Shaiju T.D, positioned at nearby firebases, approximately 30-40 meters from the target house. 


After securing the area, a final search confirmed the elimination of both the terrorists. Their bodies were identified as 
Jazim Farooq Wani and Marifat Maqbool Mangloo, operatives of the Lashkar-e-Taiba (LeT). 


This immaculately planned and clinically executed operation, which led to the neutralization of two terrorists, that 
too, without any collateral damage is testament to the indomitable courage and remarkable teamwork of team |78 CRPF, 
especially the commander Shri Teja Ram Choudhary, AC, Ct Ganesh Chand Jat and Ct Shaiju T. D. 


In this operation, S/Shri Teja Ram Choudhary, Assistant Commandant, Ganesh Chand Jat, Constable and Shaiju T.D, 
Constable Central Reserve Police Force displayed conspicuous gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a higher order.

No. 54—Pres/2025- The President of India is pleased to award Medal for Gallantry (GM) to the under mentioned 
personnel of Central Reserve Police Force: —

l. Satish Kumar Malik Second-In-Command GM 
2. Arun Kumar Second-In-Command GM

Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


On 27th February 2023, at about 900. hrs, based on specific input received from SSP PD Awantipora about the 
presence of 02 terrorists in vill Padgampora, PS Awantipora, Distt Pulwama, J&K; a CASO was launched by 30 CRPF, 55 
RR and SOG of JKP. The joint teams of QAT of 30 CRPF under command Shri Arun Kumar, 2 IC, 30 CRPF, GTT of 30 
CRPF under command Shri Akhand Pratap Singh, AC along with 55 RR and SOG of Awantipora JKP reached the target 
location and laid an initial cordon around the 35 suspected houses (including Jamia Masjid) sealing off all potential escape 
routes. 


As per the ops plan, CTT of 30 CRPF under the command of Shri Akhand Pratap Singh, AC deployed in the south- 
west direction of the target area, Shri Arun Kumar, 2 IC along with his protection party and QAT of l30 CRPF deployed 
towards north-east direction at Jhelum River and troops of SOG of JKP and 55 RR were deployed in remaining directions of 
the target area. Shri Satish Kumar Malik, 2 IC along with his protection party also reached the target location and placed in the 
north direction of the target area. One platoon each of C and-F coy of 30 CRPF and one section of D coy of !30 CRPF, E and 
G coy of |85 CRPF were also deployed in the outer cordon to deal with any emergent law and order situation. 


After sealing all escape routes, joint troops initiated a search operation. At about 0040 hrs, as the team approached 
Jamia- Masjid; hidden terrorists within the mosque initiated a heavy indiscriminate firing, targeting the search team. In- 
response, the joint troops engaged in retaliatory fire, resulting in one terrorist being hit and incapacitated on the spot, while the 
other managed to flee into a nearby house. During the exchange of fire, two personnel of 55 RR sustained injuries and were 
promptly evacuated to 92 base hospital in Srinagar. Unfortunately, one of them succumbed to his injuries and attained 
martyrdom. Moreover, a terrorist had managed to change his-position and hid in a nearby house during the first encounter. The 
operation was temporarily suspended until daylight for safety and strategic reasons. 


The operation resumed on the morning of 28th February, 2023 and joint troops commenced search of Jamia Masjid 
and recovered the dead body of terrorist Aqib Mushtaq Bhat. The second terrorist was suspected to be hidden in house No. 88, 
near Jamia Masjid. A firebase was established on the first floor of Jamia Masjid by troops overseeing the target house’s southern 
side. After repeated announcements by the J8sK police, civilians were evacuated and relocated. Subsequently, a civilian, 
equipped with a body camera, was sent into the target house and footage confirmed the presence of the terrorist hidden in the 
bathroom of the house. 


The hidden terrorist was asked to surrender but of no avail, he initiated indiscriminate firing on the troops. The troops, 
positioned in the inner cordon at the firebase, effectively retaliated. In the exchange of fire, rounds from MGL and LMG were 
directed at the bathroom door and windows of the target house. After careful assessment and detailed deliberations, two teams 
from the GRPF were formed. Team No. | led by Shri Satish Kumar Malik, 2 IC included Gt Bhagwan Sahay Gurjar with a gas 
gun and other. Team No. 2, under the command of Shri Arun Kumar, 2 IC consisted of Insp Babu Lal with a gas gun and other. 


Both the teams were briefed about the situation, but the advance posed a significant challenge for both teams. 
However, undeterred by personal safety; both teams advanced towards the target amidst continuous terrorist fire. Shri Satish 
Kumar Malik, 2 IC with team no | positioned themselves on the left side of the series of windows on the south side of the 
firebase. Despite intermittent fire from the terrorist, Shri Satish Kumar Malik, 2 IC displaying remarkable courage, moved 
beyond cover and initiated fire on the terrorist hidden in the bathroom of the target house, who was at a distance of 
approximately 50 meters. Simultaneously, Shri Arun Kumar, 2IC with team no 2 exhibited bravery and skillful tactics, taking 
charge at the right end of the series of windows. Meanwhile, troops from 55 RR fired additional rounds of LMG and MGL on 


760 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) [PART I—Sec. 


the bathroom of the target house, causing substantial damage to its doors and windows. The terrorists, however, remained 
adequately protected by the concrete wall of the bathroom against the firing of the joint troops. 


Due to the proximity of the target house to other residences, a cautious approach was essential to prevent collateral 
damage. Shri Satish Kumar Malik and Shri Arun Kumar, both 2 IC, demonstrated remarkable courage. They strategically 
exposed themselves to provide cover for their teams, facilitating the precise firing of tear gas shells at the broken door and 
window panes of the bathroom in the target house. 


Ct/ Bhagwan Sahay Guryjar on the left flank and Insp Babu Lal on the right flank, disregarding personal safety, fired 
tear gas shells in quick succession at the bathroom openings where the terrorist was hiding. Suffocated by tear gas from both 
ends of the firebase, the terrorist was compelled to abandon his position. Simultaneously, Shri Satish Kumar Malik, 2 IC, and 
Shri Arun Kumar, 2 IC positioned at both ends of the windows, seized the opportunity and bravely moved forward to effectively 
fire on the terrorist. The second terrorist was neutralized during the exchange of effective fire by the joint troops deployed at 
the firebase. Post-encounter search, another dead body of a terrorist was recovered. 


During the joint operation, two terrorists were neutralized. They were later identified as Aqib Mushtaq Bhat, Outfit 
HM, Category A and Ajaz Ahmad Bhat, Outfit JeM, Category C. Further, AK 47 Rifle 0l, AK 74 Rifle 0l, AK Series 
Magazines Op, AK live rounds |03, Pistol 0, Pistol magazines 02, Pistol rounds 20, Hand grenades 03 etc were also recovered. 


In this operation, S/Shri Satish Kumar Malik, Second-In-Command, Arun Kumar, Second-In-Command, Babu Lal, 
Inspector and Bhagwan Sahay Gurjar, Constable of Central Reserve Police Force displayed conspicuous gallantry, courage and 
devotion to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 27/02/2023.

No. 55—Pres/2025- The President of India is pleased to award Medal for Gallantry (GM) to the under mentioned 
personnel of Delhi Fire Service:— 


SI. No. Name of the Awardee Rank at the time of Action Medal awarded 
S/Shri 

l. Bhupender Prakash Assistant Divisional Officer GM 

2. Rajesh Kumar Sub-Officer GM 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


On 22nd October 2023 at around 07:40 hours, a wireless message was received at the watch room of various fire 
stations—Roop Nagar, J.R. Road, S.P. Marg, and Wazirpur—reporting a fire at H. No. 6/526, Harphool Singh Building, 
near PNB, Subzi Mandi, Ghanta Ghar. The building, comprising a ground floor and three upper stories, was engulfed in flames. 
Upon receiving the alert, four fire units were dispatched immediately. As the fire crews arrived, numerous calls flooded the 
fire control room, informing that several individuals were trapped inside and their lives were at risk. Assistant Divisional Officer 
(ADO) Bhupender Prakash was immediately deployed to the scene. Upon arrival, it was evident that the fire on the second and 
third floors was severe, with flames and smoke visible from a distance. The single staircase providing access to the upper floors 
was fully engulfed, making entry through it impossible. 


The situation was perilous, with trapped residents crying for help as the intense heat and smoke made breathing 
difficult. ADO Bhupender Prakash quickly assessed the scene and formed four teams: two for rescue and two for firefighting. 
An extension ladder was pitched to the second floor from the rear of the building to initiate rescue and firefighting operations. 
Simultaneously, ADO Bhupender Prakash and the second rescue team accessed the terrace of the burning building via an 
adjoining structure. They attempted to reach the affected floors through the terrace staircase but were blocked by thick smoke 
and extreme heat. 


Determined to save the trapped individuals, ADO Bhupender Prakash made a daring decision. He jumped from the 
terrace to the third-floor balcony, initiating a high-risk rescue operation. From the balcony, he began pulling trapped residents 
up to the terrace, where Sub-Officer Rajesh Kumar and his team were ready to assist. This perilous endeavour was both 
physically demanding and dangerous for the rescuers and the trapped individuals. Meanwhile, firefighting teams laid two 
delivery hose lines—one from the front and another from the rear to prevent the fire from spreading horizontally and vertically. 


During the operation, four LPG cylinders were recovered, one of which exploded, adding to the danger. Despite this, 
the teams continued their efforts without hesitation. The coordinated rescue and firefighting efforts resulted in the successful 


PART [-.86९०. | THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) 76 


evacuation of |6 individuals, including three men, seven women, and six children. Three of the rescued individuals sustained 
injuries and were transported to Hindu Rao Hospital for treatment. 


The bravery and leadership displayed by ADO Bhupender Prakash and Sub-Officer Rajesh Kumar was instrumental 
in the successful outcome of the operation. The heroic actions of the firefighting team led by ADO Bhupender Prakash, 
exemplified exceptional courage, teamwork, and quick thinking. His adeptness in making split second decisions under intense 
pressure ensured the safety of entrapped, illustrating an extraordinary blend of leadership and unwavering determination. Their 
innovative balcony-to- terrace rescue strategy showcased remarkable skill and dedication to public safety. The public present 
at the scene applauded their gallantry, and the next day, their heroic efforts were featured in leading newspapers. 


In this operation, S/Shri Bhupender Prakash, Assistant Divisional Officer and Rajesh Kumar, Sub-Officer of Delhi 
Fire Service displayed conspicuous gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 22/0/2023. 


(File No. 020/88/2024-PMA) 


5. M. SAMI 
Under Secretary 


No. 56—Pres/2025- The President of India is pleased to award Medal for Gallantry (GM) to the under mentioned 
personnel of Jammu & Kashmir Fire Service:— 


Sl. No. Name of the Awardee Rank at the time of Action Medal awarded 
l. Shri Noor Alam Jatla Sub-Officer GM 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


On the 2nd of July 2023, at precisely 4:47 hours, a grave and perilous fire incident unfolded at Mominabad 
Batamaloo, Srinagar. The incident involved a three-storeyed 'U’-shaped shopping complex, primarily utilized as a 
Hardware/paint/PVC items storage facility, which was tragically engulfed in flames. The situation escalated with the explosion 
of numerous cylinders within the complex and fthe fire menacingly encroached upon neighboring complexes and residential 
properties. In the face of this dire emergency, a true exemplar of bravery and dedication emerged by Sub-Officer Noor Alam 
Jatla who embodied the essence of a first responder. Without a moment's hesitation, he embarked on a valiant rescue mission, 
swiftly evacuating foe trapped individuals from the embarked on a valiant rescue mission, swiftly evacuating the trapped 
individuals from the imperilled three-storeyed shopping complex, ensuring their safety amid the chaos. Meanwhile, displaying 
remarkable professionalism, unwavering determination, and a profound commitment of duty, Sub-Officer Noor Alam Jatla 
orchestrated the deployment of personnel and firefighting equipment. In an act: of unparalleled selflessness, he ventured into 
the blazing inferno at the farthest corner of the structure armed with a charged fire hose. With unmatched precision and courage, 
he helped in successful containment and extinguishment of fire, thus averting its catastrophic spread to adjacent residential 
houses and complexes. It is with great admiration and profound respect that, in the course of his heroic actions, Sub-Officer 
Noor Alam Jatla experienced a perilous fall from the first floor of the building. After approximately 80% of the firefighting 
operation had been completed, the supervising officer found himself in a critical medical condition and grappled with the 
adverse effects of suffocation due to his unyielding efforts in dousing die flames. It is noteworthy that any minor delay, even 
of just a few minutes, in promptly transporting Sub-Officer Noor Alam Jatla to a medical facility would have posed an imminent 
risk to his life. The officer was admitted to the JVC SKIMS Hospital in Srinagar, where he received medical treatment and care 
for a duration of four days. The selfless valor and unwavering dedication exhibited by this officer serve as an inspiration. His 
actions, undertaken in the face of extreme danger, undoubtedly prevented a calamity of untold proportions. His commitment 
to preserving life and property, even at great personal risk, reflects the highest standards of professionalism. 


In this operation, Shri Noor Alam Jatla, Sub-Officer of Jammu & Kashmir Fire Service displayed conspicuous 
gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 02/07/2023. 


(File No. 020/25/2024-PMA) 


5. M. SAMI 
Under Secretary

No. 57—Pres/2025- The President of India is pleased to award Medal for Gallantry (GM) to the under mentioned 
personnel of Maharashtra Fire Service:— 


Sl. No. Name of the Awardee Rank at the time of Action Medal awarded 
S/Shri 

l. Santosh Raosaheb Ingole Senior Station Officer GM 

2. Yogesh Hanumant Kondawar Fireman GM 

3. Sunil Suresh Desale Fireman GM 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


As per CR message dated 22/09/2023 at about 502 hrs, ADFO S. G. Jaybhaye, MP-45 L/Fm crew lead by Senior 
Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole & JT-23 from Goregaon Fire station, MP-36 S.O.M.H.Sonawane L/Fm crew from 
Dindoshi Fire Station , MP-30 SO R.S.Pawade L/Fm crew, BAV-3 & JT-5 from Andheri Fire station, AWTT-6 from Malad 
Fire Station, TTL-5 from Bandra Fire Station, were turned out at above said address. 


While approaching to the scene of incident from New link road near mega mall road junction, huge dense black smoke 
was seen by first arriving officer Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole, hence accordingly message transmitted by 
him to C.R. On arrival of Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole & crew at the scene of incident, the mass of public 
were gathered at the entrance of the mall building and shouting for the help to rescue their inmates/parents who were stranded 
inside the mall building which was completely dense black smoke logged. While sensing the severity of situation and persons 
trapped inside smoke logged mall building, Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole immediately transmitted L-2 fire 
call message asking for help of 8 fire engines, water tankers and more and more TTL, ALP for urgent rescue operation. 


As per statement of first arriving officer Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole that entire mall was 
completely dense black smoke logged that even from one feet distance from entry point nothing was visible inside the mall. 
When asked by First arriving officer Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole to security personals about any other 
access/entry to the mall building then it was revealed that there are two more staircase at the back side of the mall i.e. gate no. 
2 (Eastside) & gate no.3 (Westside). He rushed towards backside i.e. south side of the mall to ensure it when he saw huge 
flames with dense black smoke were shooting out from second floor of the said mall building. He immediately instructed entire 
MP-45 crew to rush towards backside i.e. south side of the mall where he immediately layout a small hose line and a high 
pressure first aid hose line & pressed into firefighting operation by two Firemen donning BA sets. 


While approaching second floor through staircase, they came to know that about 3 to 4 persons are stranded in dense 
black smoke in the passage on first floor of the building and shouting for help to rescue them. Senior Station Officer Santosh 
Raosaheb Ingole, immediately took his two Firemen (who already donned with BA sets) and rescued 3 persons stranded in 
dense black smoke with tremendous heat and took them safely out through staircase (Gate no.2). While that rescue operation 
one person namely Mr. Raj Israni aged about 69 yrs was suffocated due to smoke inhalation and collapsed in front of them, 
that time as per instruction of Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole, to his staff members i.e. Fireman Yogesh 
Hanumant Kondawar & Fireman Sunil Suresh Desale one by one applied their BA set’s face mask to that person’s face and 
revived him (Mr. Raj Israni) by supplying fresh air/oxygen from BA set cylinder and took him out of the mall building through 
staircase. Further he was Shifted to Kokilaben Dhirubhai Ambani hospital in police van and admitted for further medical 
treatment. 


On arrival of ADFO S.G.Jaybhaye, situation was appraised to him by Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole 
and further rescue and firefighting operation was carried out under his guidance and supervision. Further Fireman Yogesh 
Hanumant Kondawar suffocated due to inhalation of smoke was removed to Balasaheb Thackrey trauma hospital in 08 
ambulance. S.O. R.S.Pawade Lfm crew of Andheri and 5.0. MH Sonawane Lfm crew of Dindoshi were informed on walkie 
talkie to reach to rear side of the mall and to layout hose line (from gate no.2) for effective firefighting work as fire was 
originated at the rear side of the mall building. Further sensing the spread of fire from second floor to third floor and persons 
still trapped on upper floors i.e. in gymnasium, hospital and on terrace L-3 fire call message was transmitted to C.R. by ADFO 
S.G.Jaybhaye asking for total |2 fire engines and more of TTLs for further rescue operation. 


Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole again joined his crew for firefighting work in a tremendous heat and 
smoke. While fighting fire in black dense smoke & tremendous heat Lfm Sandip Maruti Patil & Fm Raju Uttam Shingankar 
got suffocated due to inhalation of smoke and were removed to Cooper hospital in 08 ambulances where they were admitted 
in ICU ward by Dr. Sunil Kadam for further medical treatment. On arrival of MP-47 crew from Malad fire station, it was 
instructed to them by ADFO S.G.Jaybhaye & Senior Station Officer Santosh Raosaheb Ingole to pitch Angus ladder from west 
side of mall and rescue persons trapped inside the gymnasium on third floor of the mall. Accordingly, 03 nos. of persons were 
rescued safely through Angus ladder after severity of smoke was reduced. 


On arrival of DyCFO A.V. Bangar & DFO P.G. Dudhal situation was appraised to them and further rescue and 
firefighting operation was carried out under his guidance and supervision. Further TTL-5 from Bandra fire station was pitched 
at the front side (North) of the mall building and from there rescued safely 08 males and 0! female from terrace of the building. 
05 nos. of persons who were trapped on terrace were rescued safely through gate no.2 staircase after severity of smoke was 
reduced. Total of 08 hose lines (06 small, 0l high pressure line, 0!l TTL) of I2 fire engines were pressed in to firefighting 
operation. 


PART [-.86०. | THE GAZETTE OF INDIA, DECEMBER 6, 2025 (AGRAHAYANA I5, 947) 763 


Further CFO i/c R.N. Ambulgekar and DyCFO A.V. Parab arrived at the scene of incident 85 situation was appraised 
to them and further rescue and firefighting operation was carried out under their guidance and supervision. Thorough search 
was made in entire mall for any trapped persons in presence of police. In this way total about 20 nos. of persons (9 male & 0 
female) were rescued safely who were trapped inside the dense smoke logged mall building. Further it was observed that fire 
was confined to electric wiring, electric cables, electric duct, A.C. duct, electric installation, fixtures, various office furniture, 
various office records , valuable documents, personal belongings, office papers , electrical gadgets, Personal computers & its 
accessories, printers, laptops & hardware equipments, split AC indoor & outdoor units, studio equipments , movie film 
discs/tracks , important film licenses & agreement papers, Income tax documents, Television sets , cameras, various decorative 
materials, gymnasium equipments, CCTV & its accessories, UPS sets in server room, main wooden doors with its frame, glass 
doors , glass partitions, false ceilings, paint & plaster work , polycarbonate roof sheets in about in an area about 5000 sq. fts. 
in various shops, offices, gymnasium on first, second & third floor of said commercial mall building. 


In this operation, S/Shri Santosh Raosaheb Ingole, Senior Station Officer, Yogesh Hanumant Kondawar, Fireman and 
Sunil Suresh Desale, Fireman of Maharashtra Fire Service displayed conspicuous gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a 
higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. !024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 22/09/2023.

No. 58—Pres/2025- The President of India is pleased to award Medal for Gallantry (GM) to the under mentioned 
personnel of Uttar Pradesh Home Guards and Civil Defence Service:— 


Sl. No. Name of the Awardee Rank at the time of Action Medal awarded 
l. Shri Amarnath Misra Chief Warden GM 


Statement of service for which the decoration has been awarded 


Shri Amarnath Misra is a Chief warden of Civil Defence, Lucknow. He has participated and organized many disasters 
related training programs in district level and National level. A disastrous situation at Yahiyaganj Bazar, Lucknow on 
08/09/2024 due to short circuit a fire incident occured in a house situated at fourth floor of a building. The owner Sudhaker 
Bajpai, his brother Diwaker Bajpai and family started panicking. Yahiyaganj Bazar was over crowded area and this area has 
very narrow roads for escape route. Shri Amarnath Misra immediately responded to the incident place and started evacuating 
the area. After that with the help of some local volunteers, he cordoned off the area. Then, he immediately rushed towards the 
fourth floor of the building where the fire incident occured. He cut off the electric supply of the building from the main line 
and evacuated all members of Sudhaker's family from incident place to safe place outside the building. The room where this 
fire started, was full of poisonous smoke. He put wet handkerchief on his nose and enter the house. He found fire in the kitchen 
area where two LPG gas cylinders was near the fire. If cylinders caught fire would be more devastating. He immediately put 
both cylinders out of the house in safe place and he started throwing water with the help of a bucket on the fire. Sooner he 
extinguished fire in the house. Due to Shri Amarnath Misra's alertness and quick decision-making ability, this fire incident was 
controlled very soon with not any injuries or casualties. 


In this operation, Shri Amarnath Misra, Chief Warden of Uttar Pradesh Home Guards and Civil Defence Service 
displayed conspicuous gallantry, courage and devotion to duty of a higher order. 


The Medal for Gallantry (GM) is conferred under President’s Secretariat Notification No. 85-Pres/2023 dated 
l4th October, 2023 and Ministry of Home Affairs’ communication No. |!024/06/2023-PMA dated l6th October, 2023. 
Consequently, this award carries with it the admissible monetary allowance with effect from 08/09/2024. 


(File No. 020/96/2024-PMA) 


5. M. SAMI 
Under Secretary
